The Department of Food and Public Distribution's portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that a guiding principle of the Act is its life-cycle approach, under which special provisions entitle pregnant women, lactating mothers and children from six months to fourteen years to receive a nutritious meal free of cost through the anganwadi network under the Integrated Child Development Services scheme and through schools under the mid-day meal scheme, that higher nutritional norms are prescribed for malnourished children up to six years of age, and that pregnant women and lactating mothers are entitled to cash maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees to partly compensate wage loss and supplement nutrition; the same source records that the eldest woman of the household aged eighteen or above is mandated to be the head of the household for the purpose of issuing ration cards.
B. The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.
C. The same official portal records that where entitled quantities of foodgrains or meals are not supplied, the entitled person is entitled to receive a food security allowance from the concerned State Government, payable to each person within the time and manner prescribed by the Central Government, and that these provisions are governed through the Food Security Allowance Rules, 2015; this is the clause that makes the entitlement a rights-based claim rather than a discretionary scheme, and citing it by name is what allows an answer to argue the charity-to-claim shift with evidence instead of assertion.
D. The cycle recorded in the owner runs from income and asset deprivation to food insecurity, from food insecurity to undernutrition in the form of both calorie deficit and protein and micronutrient deficit, from undernutrition to impaired cognitive, physical and immune development, from impaired development to low human-capital formation in schooling attainment and productivity, and from low productivity back to low earnings, which reproduces the deprivation in the next generation; naming the loop and the point of intergenerational transmission is what converts a scheme list into the explanation that the 2024 demand asks for.

**Answer: D.**
**Explanation:** The cycle recorded in the owner runs from income and asset deprivation to food insecurity, from food insecurity to undernutrition in the form of both calorie deficit and protein and micronutrient deficit, from undernutrition to impaired cognitive, physical and immune development, from impaired development to low human-capital formation in schooling attainment and productivity, and from low productivity back to low earnings, which reproduces the deprivation in the next generation; naming the loop and the point of intergenerational transmission is what converts a scheme list into the explanation that the 2024 demand asks for. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q9. Which statement correctly identifies Food security against nutrition security?

A. Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one.
B. The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion.
C. Stunting is low height-for-age in children under five and reflects cumulative chronic nutritional and health deprivation, especially in the first thousand days, wasting is low weight-for-height in children under five and reflects recent or acute food shortage or illness and is therefore a crisis indicator, and anaemia is a haemoglobin deficiency arising from iron, folate or vitamin B12 shortfall that reduces oxygen-carrying capacity and is prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women; the three call for different policy responses, so treating them as interchangeable is a factual error rather than a stylistic one.
D. The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.

**Answer: A.**
**Explanation:** Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q13. Which statement correctly identifies Stunting, wasting and anaemia as distinct indicators?

A. Stunting is low height-for-age in children under five and reflects cumulative chronic nutritional and health deprivation, especially in the first thousand days, wasting is low weight-for-height in children under five and reflects recent or acute food shortage or illness and is therefore a crisis indicator, and anaemia is a haemoglobin deficiency arising from iron, folate or vitamin B12 shortfall that reduces oxygen-carrying capacity and is prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women; the three call for different policy responses, so treating them as interchangeable is a factual error rather than a stylistic one.

**Explanation:** The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q21. Which statement correctly identifies Central Issue Price ceiling against the free-distribution policy?

A. The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic.
B. The same official portal records that where entitled quantities of foodgrains or meals are not supplied, the entitled person is entitled to receive a food security allowance from the concerned State Government, payable to each person within the time and manner prescribed by the Central Government, and that these provisions are governed through the Food Security Allowance Rules, 2015; this is the clause that makes the entitlement a rights-based claim rather than a discretionary scheme, and citing it by name is what allows an answer to argue the charity-to-claim shift with evidence instead of assertion.
C. The official portal records that the Act defines a joint responsibility, under which the Centre allocates the required foodgrains to States and Union Territories, transports them up to designated depots in each State and Union Territory and provides central assistance for delivery from designated Food Corporation of India godowns to the doorstep of the fair-price shop, while States and Union Territories are responsible for effective implementation, which includes identifying eligible households, issuing ration cards and distributing the foodgrain entitlement; this division is the reason why a delivery failure must be attributed to a named tier rather than to the government in general.
D. The Department of Food and Public Distribution's portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that a guiding principle of the Act is its life-cycle approach, under which special provisions entitle pregnant women, lactating mothers and children from six months to fourteen years to receive a nutritious meal free of cost through the anganwadi network under the Integrated Child Development Services scheme and through schools under the mid-day meal scheme, that higher nutritional norms are prescribed for malnourished children up to six years of age, and that pregnant women and lactating mothers are entitled to cash maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees to partly compensate wage loss and supplement nutrition; the same source records that the eldest woman of the household aged eighteen or above is mandated to be the head of the household for the purpose of issuing ration cards.

**Answer: A.**
**Explanation:** The Act itself fixes Central Issue Prices as a statutory ceiling of three rupees per kilogram for rice, two rupees per kilogram for wheat and one rupee per kilogram for coarse grains, but since 1 January 2023 the Pradhan Mantri Garib Kalyan Anna Yojana has been merged with the National Food Security Act and all Act foodgrains are distributed free of cost to Antyodaya Anna Yojana and priority-household beneficiaries, a policy the Union Cabinet extended for five years through December 2028; the discipline this demands is exact, because the statutory ceiling and the current distribution policy are two different facts and stating the ceiling as the price actually paid is the single commonest error in this topic. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

Severe acute malnutrition is defined by weight-for-height below minus three standard deviations, is severe wasting and requires facility-based therapeutic care at a nutrition rehabilitation centre, whereas moderate acute malnutrition lies between minus two and minus three standard deviations and can be managed through community-based supplementary feeding, and both are tracked through anganwadi growth monitoring; the examinable point is that acute malnutrition needs screening, referral, treatment where indicated and community follow-up, so an additional foodgrain allocation is not a response to it.
B. Nutrition-specific interventions directly target nutritional outcomes through supplementary feeding, micronutrient supplementation, growth monitoring and breastfeeding promotion, whereas nutrition-sensitive interventions address the underlying determinants through other sectors, including water, sanitation and hygiene, agricultural diversification, social protection and women's education, and act on nutrition indirectly; the convergence argument in a Mains answer rests entirely on this distinction, because it explains why stunting reduction cannot be procured from the food ministry alone.
C. The owners record the National Family Health Survey as the primary survey source for stunting, wasting, anaemia and related household outcomes and require the round to be retained beside every value: the fifth round of 2019-21 is recorded as the dated baseline with 35.5 per cent of children under five stunted, 19.3 per cent wasted and 67.1 per cent of children aged six to fifty-nine months anaemic nationally, and the sixth round of 2023-24, released on 29 May 2026, is recorded as the current national round with under-five stunting reported at 29.3 per cent, a survey covering 715 districts and about 6.79 lakh households; the rule stated by the owners is that indicators from the two rounds must never be combined without naming each round.
D. Income or calorie poverty, multidimensional poverty and anthropometry measure different deprivations, so falling monetary poverty can coexist with continuing hunger and undernutrition, and this divergence is the analytical core of the demand that asks about poverty and hunger moving apart alongside shrinking social expenditure; the answer route is to separate availability, access, absorption and utilisation, because availability failures are procurement problems, access failures are entitlement and portability problems, and absorption failures are health, sanitation and disease problems that no grain allocation can reach.

**Answer: B.**
**Explanation:** Nutrition-specific interventions directly target nutritional outcomes through supplementary feeding, micronutrient supplementation, growth monitoring and breastfeeding promotion, whereas nutrition-sensitive interventions address the underlying determinants through other sectors, including water, sanitation and hygiene, agricultural diversification, social protection and women's education, and act on nutrition indirectly; the convergence argument in a Mains answer rests entirely on this distinction, because it explains why stunting reduction cannot be procured from the food ministry alone. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

### Q57. Which statement correctly identifies Take-home ration against the hot cooked meal?

A. A take-home ration is a dry ration given to the beneficiary to cook at home and is used for pregnant and lactating women and for children between six months and three years, whereas a hot cooked meal is served at the anganwadi centre or the school, so the hot meal guarantees consumption at the point of delivery but requires kitchen and staffing infrastructure, while the take-home ration is more flexible and may be redistributed within the household; naming this trade-off is how an answer explains a delivery design choice rather than merely listing it.

C. D. **Answer: A.**

### Q76. Which statement avoids a close-option trap about The intervention bank for breaking the cycle?

The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion.
B. Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one.
C. Stunting is low height-for-age in children under five and reflects cumulative chronic nutritional and health deprivation, especially in the first thousand days, wasting is low weight-for-height in children under five and reflects recent or acute food shortage or illness and is therefore a crisis indicator, and anaemia is a haemoglobin deficiency arising from iron, folate or vitamin B12 shortfall that reduces oxygen-carrying capacity and is prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women; the three call for different policy responses, so treating them as interchangeable is a factual error rather than a stylistic one.
D. The owner's breaking-the-cycle levers are the first-thousand-days focus delivered through the nutrition mission and the anganwadi system, school nutrition through PM POSHAN, portability for migrants through One Nation One Ration Card, women's nutrition and anaemia reduction aimed at adolescent girls and pregnant and lactating women through the Anaemia Mukt Bharat strategy with its six-by-six-by-six design, and convergence with water, sanitation and hygiene because sanitation and safe water govern nutrient absorption; the owner also records that fortification can address selected micronutrient gaps at scale but cannot replace dietary diversity, infection control, safe water or clinical treatment.

**Answer: D.**
**Explanation:** The owner's breaking-the-cycle levers are the first-thousand-days focus delivered through the nutrition mission and the anganwadi system, school nutrition through PM POSHAN, portability for migrants through One Nation One Ration Card, women's nutrition and anaemia reduction aimed at adolescent girls and pregnant and lactating women through the Anaemia Mukt Bharat strategy with its six-by-six-by-six design, and convergence with water, sanitation and hygiene because sanitation and safe water govern nutrient absorption; the owner also records that fortification can address selected micronutrient gaps at scale but cannot replace dietary diversity, infection control, safe water or clinical treatment. The remaining options belong to different chronology, actor or analytical categories.

Three General Studies Mains demands and two objective demands are routed to this topic in the audited routing ledgers, and each Mains demand is reproduced below as a demand card with its printed year, paper, question number, directive, marks and word limit exactly as the ledgers record them: 2018 General Studies Paper II Question 17, 2019 General Studies Paper II Question 7 and 2024 General Studies Paper II Question 7. Two objective demands are also routed, namely 2018 Prelims General Studies Paper I Question 25 on the beneficiary categories and provisions of the National Food Security Act, 2013 and 2023 Prelims General Studies Paper I Question 52 on the interventions and target groups of the Anaemia Mukt Bharat strategy; the audited ledger records that the official 2018-2023 Prelims keys are not held locally, so no option, key or inferred answer is recorded for either objective demand and neither is converted into a solved question. The locally held OCR-searchable official General Studies papers were read only to confirm the printed wording of the routed Mains demands; no question was invented from them and no marking scheme or page precision was imported. Every model solution below is original work written to the repository's examiner-grade standard using the claim, named evidence, analysis and qualification pattern.

3. **Claim and named evidence:** Map rights-holder, household/community context, duty-bearer, frontline worker, provider and accountability forum. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
4. **Claim and named evidence:** Trace identification, eligibility, documentation, finance, access, portability, grievance, appeal and correction. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
5. **Claim and named evidence:** Use a named Indian law, judgment, scheme, institution or official dataset with source, date, denominator and status. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.
6. **Claim and named evidence:** Test intersectionality, regional variation, stigma, accessibility, privacy, fiscal adequacy, exclusion and causal limits. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.

**Counter-position / limit:** A constitutional promise, prohibition, reservation provision, scheme catalogue, allocation, registration count or headline statistic cannot alone establish accessible implementation, adequate benefit, dignity, remedy or attributable outcome; test eligibility, institutions, frontline capacity, intersectionality, regional variation, grievance and evidence status.

**Qualified conclusion:** The answer must resolve the Social Justice demand in “VERIFIED PYQ OWNERSHIP AUDIT”.

**Executable exam-length answer / compression plan:** For 15 marks, spend one-sixth of the time decoding the directive and drawing right/need → institution → eligibility → delivery → outcome → remedy; state a thesis; write four to seven claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification points; reserve the final minute for dignity, accessibility, portability, grievance, denominator, fiscal and causal limits.

#### 3. Detailed causal chain: the intergenerational trap (2024 PYQ Q7)

1. **Poverty** limits household capacity to purchase diverse, nutrient-dense food
   and invest in health/sanitation.
2. **Food insecurity** manifests as calorie deficit (hunger) and dietary
   monotony (lack of micronutrients).
3. **Undernutrition in early childhood** (especially first 1000 days) causes
   stunting (chronic) and wasting (acute), impairs cognitive development and
   weakens immunity.
4. **Impaired development** reduces school attendance, learning outcomes and
   skill acquisition.
5. **Low human capital** translates into low productivity and earnings in
   adulthood.
6. **Low earnings** perpetuate household poverty, which is then transmitted to
   the next generation (intergenerational trap).
7. **Breaking-the-cycle levers:**
   - Nutrition-specific: Poshan 2.0 (first-1000-days focus), PM POSHAN (school-
     age), micronutrient supplementation (IFA tablets).
   - Nutrition-sensitive: WASH convergence (SBM, JJM), income support (DBT,
     social security), agricultural diversification.
   - Portability: ONORC for migrant-worker inclusion.

#### 10. PYQ-based analytical application

- ✅ **2024 Q7 (10 marks):** A strong answer should:
  1. Diagram the vicious cycle (poverty -> undernutrition -> impaired development
     -> low productivity -> poverty).
  2. Distinguish nutrition-specific (Poshan 2.0, PM POSHAN, micronutrient
     supplementation) from nutrition-sensitive (WASH, income support, agriculture)
     interventions.
  3. Emphasise the first-1000-days window and women's nutrition (anaemia in
     adolescent girls and PLW).
  4. Cite ONORC portability for migrant inclusion.
  5. Conclude with the convergence imperative: no single scheme breaks the cycle.

- ✅ **2025 GS-II Q16 (15 marks, 250 words):** Build the “paradox of poverty” as a
  self-reinforcing asset-capability loop:

```text
UNEQUAL OWNERSHIP OF LAND / CAPITAL / CREDIT / DIGITAL ACCESS
                         |
                         v
       unequal opportunity and bargaining power
                         |
                         v
             low capability and income
                         |
                         v
       weak saving, investment and asset formation
                         |
                         +----> inherited ownership inequality
```

  Separate productive-asset reform (land records/tenancy security, credit, skills,
  commons and digital access) from necessary but non-transformative consumption relief.

### PYQ DEMAND CARD 1 — 2018 General Studies Paper II Question 17

**Demand:** How far do you agree that a focus on the availability of food rather than on human development policies is the cause of hunger in India, 15 marks, 250 words, exactly as recorded in the audited 2018-2023 Mains routing ledger.

**Status:** Routed to this owner in the audited 2018-2023 Mains routing ledger, where the Core route is recorded as superseding the older Advanced ownership. No official answer key exists for a Mains demand and none is claimed.

**Model solution:** Claim: the availability argument is partly right and structurally incomplete, because availability is only the first of four stages and hunger in India is produced mainly at the access and absorption stages. Named evidence and example: the statutory entitlement of five kilograms per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households under the National Food Security Act, 2013, with the Department of Food and Public Distribution portal read on 2 September 2026 recording enactment on 5 July 2013, coverage of up to seventy-five per cent of the rural and fifty per cent of the urban population and a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons; the Act's life-cycle provisions and the maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees; the distinction between nutrition-specific and nutrition-sensitive interventions; and the National Family Health Survey record of under-five stunting at 35.5 per cent in the fifth round of 2019-21 and 29.3 per cent in the sixth round of 2023-24 released on 29 May 2026. Analysis: an availability-first reading explains procurement and buffer policy but cannot explain why a household holding a valid card remains undernourished, since access fails through identification, documentation and migration barriers, and absorption fails through diarrhoeal disease, unsafe water and poor sanitation, which is precisely why the reduction in stunting between two named rounds cannot be credited to grain allocation alone. Qualification: the human-development reading must not be pushed into denying that availability matters, because a chronic supply failure would make every downstream policy irrelevant, and each indicator must be carried with its survey round. Why this earns marks: it takes a position on a how-far-do-you-agree directive, uses the four-stage frame as the organising device and closes with a bounded verdict.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **answer** requires a direct position on “PYQ DEMAND CARD 1 — 2018 General Studies Paper II Question 17”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

**Detailed examiner-grade model answer:**

**Introduction and thesis:** Claim: the availability argument is partly right and structurally incomplete, because availability is only the first of four stages and hunger in India is produced mainly at the access and absorption stages. Named evidence and example: the statutory entitlement of five kilograms per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households under the National Food Security Act, 2013, with the Department of Food and Public Distribution portal read on 2 September 2026 recording enactment on 5 July 2013, coverage of up to seventy-five per cent of the rural and fifty per cent of the urban population and a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons; the Act's life-cycle provisions and the maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees; the distinction between nutrition-specific and nutrition-sensitive interventions; and the National Family Health Survey record of under-five stunting at 35.5 per cent in the fifth round of 2019-21 and 29.3 per cent in the sixth round of 2023-24 released on 29 May 2026. Analysis: an availability-first reading explains procurement and buffer policy but cannot explain why a household holding a valid card remains undernourished, since access fails through identification, documentation and migration barriers, and absorption fails through diarrhoeal disease, unsafe water and poor sanitation, which is precisely why the reduction in stunting between two named rounds cannot be credited to grain allocation alone. Qualification: the human-development reading must not be pushed into denying that availability matters, because a chronic supply failure would make every downstream policy irrelevant, and each indicator must be carried with its survey round. Why this earns marks: it takes a position on a how-far-do-you-agree directive, uses the four-stage frame as the organising device and closes with a bounded verdict.

### PYQ DEMAND CARD 2 — 2019 General Studies Paper II Question 7

**Demand:** Elucidate the relationship between the divergence of poverty and hunger and shrinking social expenditure, 10 marks, 150 words, exactly as recorded in the audited 2018-2023 Mains routing ledger.

**Status:** Routed to this owner in the audited 2018-2023 Mains routing ledger, where the Core route is recorded as superseding the older Advanced ownership. No official answer key exists for a Mains demand and none is claimed.

**Model solution:** Claim: poverty and hunger diverge because they are measured by different instruments and repaired by different expenditures, so a fall in one is not evidence of a fall in the other. Named evidence and example: the owner's rule that income or calorie poverty, multidimensional poverty and anthropometry measure different deprivations; the four-stage availability, access, absorption and utilisation frame; the anganwadi service layer under Poshan 2.0 with the Ministry of Women and Child Development and the school layer under PM POSHAN with the Ministry of Education; and the first thousand days from conception to age two as the window in which deprivation becomes irreversible. Analysis: monetary poverty responds to income, transfer and employment expenditure, whereas hunger and undernutrition respond to service expenditure on anganwadi staffing, growth monitoring, therapeutic care, water and sanitation, so compressing the service head while transfers hold produces exactly the observed divergence, and because the critical window is short, a service compression today is recorded in the anthropometry of the next decade. Qualification: no expenditure figure or trend is asserted here, since budget numbers must be cited with their year and head, and the divergence claim itself must be carried with the survey rounds compared. Why this earns marks: it elucidates a mechanism connecting two measurements to two expenditure heads instead of describing poverty in general.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **answer** requires a direct position on “PYQ DEMAND CARD 2 — 2019 General Studies Paper II Question 7”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

### PYQ DEMAND CARD 3 — 2024 General Studies Paper II Question 7

**Demand:** Poverty and malnutrition create a vicious cycle, adversely affecting human capital formation. What steps can be taken to break the cycle, explain, 10 marks, 150 words, exactly as recorded in the audited 2024-2025 Mains routing ledger and reproduced in the Basic owner.

**Status:** Routed to this owner in the audited 2024-2025 Mains routing ledger. No official answer key exists for a Mains demand and none is claimed.

**Model solution:** Claim: the cycle is intergenerational, so it is broken only at the points where deprivation becomes irreversible and where entitlement fails to travel with the person. Named evidence and example: the loop from income and asset deprivation through calorie and micronutrient deficit to impaired cognitive, physical and immune development, low human capital and low earnings; the first thousand days from conception to age two; Poshan 2.0 and the anganwadi layer under the Ministry of Women and Child Development; PM POSHAN in Classes I to VIII under the Ministry of Education; One Nation One Ration Card portability using Aadhaar-seeded cards and electronic point-of-sale machines; and the national anaemia strategy with its six-by-six-by-six design for adolescent girls and pregnant and lactating women. Analysis: the first thousand days fix the sequencing, because a stunted child becomes a lower-earning adult whose child is again undernourished, so maternal and adolescent nutrition are child-nutrition instruments rather than separate heads; portability addresses the exclusion produced by migration; and convergence with water, sanitation and hygiene addresses absorption, which no additional grain allocation can reach. Qualification: acute malnutrition is not answered by extra grain, since severe acute malnutrition at weight-for-height below minus three standard deviations requires facility-based therapeutic care, and every outcome cited must carry its survey round, with under-five stunting recorded at 35.5 per cent in the fifth round of 2019-21 and 29.3 per cent in the sixth round of 2023-24. Why this earns marks: it explains a loop, attaches each lever to the exact link it cuts and ends with a treatment and data qualification.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **answer** requires a direct position on “PYQ DEMAND CARD 3 — 2024 General Studies Paper II Question 7”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 1 — 10 MARKS

**Question:** Explain how poverty and malnutrition reinforce each other and identify the interventions that can break the cycle. Answer in about 150 words.

**Model thesis:** The loop is intergenerational and multi-causal, so the answer must trace deprivation through undernutrition, impaired development and low human capital back to low earnings, then attach named multi-sectoral levers rather than a single-scheme remedy.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- The cycle recorded in the owner runs from income and asset deprivation to food insecurity, from food insecurity to undernutrition in the form of both calorie deficit and protein and micronutrient deficit, from undernutrition to impaired cognitive, physical and immune development, from impaired development to low human-capital formation in schooling attainment and productivity, and from low productivity back to low earnings, which reproduces the deprivation in the next generation; naming the loop and the point of intergenerational transmission is what converts a scheme list into the explanation that the 2024 demand asks for.
- The window from conception to the age of two is the period during which nutritional deprivation causes irreversible stunting and cognitive impairment, so interventions placed inside it yield the highest return, and this is the reason the nutrition mission is designed around pregnancy, lactation and the child under two rather than around the school-age child alone; in an answer, the window supplies the sequencing argument, because it explains why adolescent-girl and maternal nutrition are treated as child-nutrition instruments rather than as separate welfare heads.
- The owner's breaking-the-cycle levers are the first-thousand-days focus delivered through the nutrition mission and the anganwadi system, school nutrition through PM POSHAN, portability for migrants through One Nation One Ration Card, women's nutrition and anaemia reduction aimed at adolescent girls and pregnant and lactating women through the Anaemia Mukt Bharat strategy with its six-by-six-by-six design, and convergence with water, sanitation and hygiene because sanitation and safe water govern nutrient absorption; the owner also records that fortification can address selected micronutrient gaps at scale but cannot replace dietary diversity, infection control, safe water or clinical treatment.
- Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one.

**Qualified conclusion:** The loop is intergenerational and multi-causal, so the answer must trace deprivation through undernutrition, impaired development and low human capital back to low earnings, then attach named multi-sectoral levers rather than a single-scheme remedy.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **explain** requires a direct position on “Explain how poverty and malnutrition reinforce each other and identify the interventions that…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 2 — 10 MARKS

**Question:** Elucidate why measured poverty and measured hunger can move in different directions. Answer in about 150 words.

**Model thesis:** Different instruments measure different deprivations, so the answer must separate monetary poverty, multidimensional poverty and anthropometry, then separate availability, access, absorption and utilisation to locate where the divergence is produced.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- Income or calorie poverty, multidimensional poverty and anthropometry measure different deprivations, so falling monetary poverty can coexist with continuing hunger and undernutrition, and this divergence is the analytical core of the demand that asks about poverty and hunger moving apart alongside shrinking social expenditure; the answer route is to separate availability, access, absorption and utilisation, because availability failures are procurement problems, access failures are entitlement and portability problems, and absorption failures are health, sanitation and disease problems that no grain allocation can reach.
- Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one.
- Stunting is low height-for-age in children under five and reflects cumulative chronic nutritional and health deprivation, especially in the first thousand days, wasting is low weight-for-height in children under five and reflects recent or acute food shortage or illness and is therefore a crisis indicator, and anaemia is a haemoglobin deficiency arising from iron, folate or vitamin B12 shortfall that reduces oxygen-carrying capacity and is prevalent among children, adolescent girls and pregnant women; the three call for different policy responses, so treating them as interchangeable is a factual error rather than a stylistic one.
- The owners record the National Family Health Survey as the primary survey source for stunting, wasting, anaemia and related household outcomes and require the round to be retained beside every value: the fifth round of 2019-21 is recorded as the dated baseline with 35.5 per cent of children under five stunted, 19.3 per cent wasted and 67.1 per cent of children aged six to fifty-nine months anaemic nationally, and the sixth round of 2023-24, released on 29 May 2026, is recorded as the current national round with under-five stunting reported at 29.3 per cent, a survey covering 715 districts and about 6.79 lakh households; the rule stated by the owners is that indicators from the two rounds must never be combined without naming each round.

**Qualified conclusion:** Different instruments measure different deprivations, so the answer must separate monetary poverty, multidimensional poverty and anthropometry, then separate availability, access, absorption and utilisation to locate where the divergence is produced.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **answer** requires a direct position on “Elucidate why measured poverty and measured hunger can move in different directions. Answer…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 3 — 15 MARKS

**Question:** Assess whether India's food-security architecture has converted a discretionary scheme into an enforceable entitlement. Answer in about 250 words.

**Model thesis:** The statutory shift is real and is evidenced by the entitlement quantities, the life-cycle provisions and the food security allowance, but coverage identification, portability gaps and absorption failures limit it, so the assessment must be graded and must name the tier at which the claim fails.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion.
- The Department of Food and Public Distribution's portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that a guiding principle of the Act is its life-cycle approach, under which special provisions entitle pregnant women, lactating mothers and children from six months to fourteen years to receive a nutritious meal free of cost through the anganwadi network under the Integrated Child Development Services scheme and through schools under the mid-day meal scheme, that higher nutritional norms are prescribed for malnourished children up to six years of age, and that pregnant women and lactating mothers are entitled to cash maternity benefit of not less than six thousand rupees to partly compensate wage loss and supplement nutrition; the same source records that the eldest woman of the household aged eighteen or above is mandated to be the head of the household for the purpose of issuing ration cards.
- The same official portal records that where entitled quantities of foodgrains or meals are not supplied, the entitled person is entitled to receive a food security allowance from the concerned State Government, payable to each person within the time and manner prescribed by the Central Government, and that these provisions are governed through the Food Security Allowance Rules, 2015; this is the clause that makes the entitlement a rights-based claim rather than a discretionary scheme, and citing it by name is what allows an answer to argue the charity-to-claim shift with evidence instead of assertion.
- The official portal records that the Act defines a joint responsibility, under which the Centre allocates the required foodgrains to States and Union Territories, transports them up to designated depots in each State and Union Territory and provides central assistance for delivery from designated Food Corporation of India godowns to the doorstep of the fair-price shop, while States and Union Territories are responsible for effective implementation, which includes identifying eligible households, issuing ration cards and distributing the foodgrain entitlement; this division is the reason why a delivery failure must be attributed to a named tier rather than to the government in general.

**Qualified conclusion:** The statutory shift is real and is evidenced by the entitlement quantities, the life-cycle provisions and the food security allowance, but coverage identification, portability gaps and absorption failures limit it, so the assessment must be graded and must name the tier at which the claim fails.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **assess** requires a direct position on “Assess whether India's food-security architecture has converted a discretionary scheme into…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

**Detailed examiner-grade model answer:**

**Introduction and thesis:** The statutory shift is real and is evidenced by the entitlement quantities, the life-cycle provisions and the food security allowance, but coverage identification, portability gaps and absorption failures limit it, so the assessment must be graded and must name the tier at which the claim fails.

1. **Claim and named evidence:** The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 4 — 15 MARKS

**Question:** Examine the claim that a focus on food availability rather than on human-development policy is the principal cause of hunger in India. Answer in about 250 words.

**Model thesis:** Availability is only the first of four stages, so the examination must test the claim against access, absorption and utilisation, concede what the availability argument does explain, and end by naming the conversion failures that grain allocation cannot reach.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- Food security is reliable access to sufficient, safe and nutritious food to meet dietary needs and is addressed in India primarily through the statutory foodgrain entitlement, which is a calorie and grain-access instrument, whereas nutrition security goes beyond calorie access to include dietary diversity, micronutrients, care practices and the health and sanitation environment and is therefore multi-sectoral; the examinable consequence is that the public distribution system can be working exactly as designed while nutrition outcomes stagnate, so the two must never be argued as one.
- Nutrition-specific interventions directly target nutritional outcomes through supplementary feeding, micronutrient supplementation, growth monitoring and breastfeeding promotion, whereas nutrition-sensitive interventions address the underlying determinants through other sectors, including water, sanitation and hygiene, agricultural diversification, social protection and women's education, and act on nutrition indirectly; the convergence argument in a Mains answer rests entirely on this distinction, because it explains why stunting reduction cannot be procured from the food ministry alone.
- The owner's breaking-the-cycle levers are the first-thousand-days focus delivered through the nutrition mission and the anganwadi system, school nutrition through PM POSHAN, portability for migrants through One Nation One Ration Card, women's nutrition and anaemia reduction aimed at adolescent girls and pregnant and lactating women through the Anaemia Mukt Bharat strategy with its six-by-six-by-six design, and convergence with water, sanitation and hygiene because sanitation and safe water govern nutrient absorption; the owner also records that fortification can address selected micronutrient gaps at scale but cannot replace dietary diversity, infection control, safe water or clinical treatment.
- Severe acute malnutrition is defined by weight-for-height below minus three standard deviations, is severe wasting and requires facility-based therapeutic care at a nutrition rehabilitation centre, whereas moderate acute malnutrition lies between minus two and minus three standard deviations and can be managed through community-based supplementary feeding, and both are tracked through anganwadi growth monitoring; the examinable point is that acute malnutrition needs screening, referral, treatment where indicated and community follow-up, so an additional foodgrain allocation is not a response to it.

**Qualified conclusion:** Availability is only the first of four stages, so the examination must test the claim against access, absorption and utilisation, concede what the availability argument does explain, and end by naming the conversion failures that grain allocation cannot reach.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **examine** requires a direct position on “Examine the claim that a focus on food availability rather than on human-development policy…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 5 — 20 MARKS

**Question:** Assess the design of India's nutrition delivery system across the life cycle and the trade-offs it embodies. Answer in about 300 words.

**Model thesis:** The system layers a statutory grain entitlement, an anganwadi service layer and a school-meal layer across different ages and ministries, so the assessment must test each layer against the outcome it claims, name the ration-format and ministry trade-offs, and close with a graded verdict.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- Poshan 2.0, also carried as Saksham Anganwadi, is the umbrella nutrition programme administered by the Ministry of Women and Child Development that merges the Integrated Child Development Services and related schemes and delivers supplementary nutrition as take-home ration and hot cooked meals, growth monitoring through weight and height tracking, pre-school education, immunisation referral and health and nutrition counselling through anganwadi centres, with its primary focus on children under six and on pregnant and lactating women and an explicit emphasis on the first thousand days from conception to age two.
- PM POSHAN, the erstwhile mid-day meal programme, is administered by the Ministry of Education and implemented through State education departments and school management committees, providing a cooked meal to children in government and government-aided schools in Classes I to VIII with the stated objectives of attendance, retention and improved nutritional status; the ministry pairing is a standing objective-question trap, because Poshan 2.0 sits with the Ministry of Women and Child Development while PM POSHAN sits with the Ministry of Education, and the two are complementary layers for different age groups rather than duplicates.
- A take-home ration is a dry ration given to the beneficiary to cook at home and is used for pregnant and lactating women and for children between six months and three years, whereas a hot cooked meal is served at the anganwadi centre or the school, so the hot meal guarantees consumption at the point of delivery but requires kitchen and staffing infrastructure, while the take-home ration is more flexible and may be redistributed within the household; naming this trade-off is how an answer explains a delivery design choice rather than merely listing it.
- Severe acute malnutrition is defined by weight-for-height below minus three standard deviations, is severe wasting and requires facility-based therapeutic care at a nutrition rehabilitation centre, whereas moderate acute malnutrition lies between minus two and minus three standard deviations and can be managed through community-based supplementary feeding, and both are tracked through anganwadi growth monitoring; the examinable point is that acute malnutrition needs screening, referral, treatment where indicated and community follow-up, so an additional foodgrain allocation is not a response to it.

**Qualified conclusion:** The system layers a statutory grain entitlement, an anganwadi service layer and a school-meal layer across different ages and ministries, so the assessment must test each layer against the outcome it claims, name the ration-format and ministry trade-offs, and close with a graded verdict.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **assess** requires a direct position on “Assess the design of India's nutrition delivery system across the life cycle and the trade-…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

**Detailed examiner-grade model answer:**

**Introduction and thesis:** The system layers a statutory grain entitlement, an anganwadi service layer and a school-meal layer across different ages and ministries, so the assessment must test each layer against the outcome it claims, name the ration-format and ministry trade-offs, and close with a graded verdict.

**Analytical body:**

1. **Claim and named evidence:** Poshan 2.0, also carried as Saksham Anganwadi, is the umbrella nutrition programme administered by the Ministry of Women and Child Development that merges the Integrated Child Development Services and related schemes and delivers supplementary nutrition as take-home ration and hot cooked meals, growth monitoring through weight and height tracking, pre-school education, immunisation referral and health and nutrition counselling through anganwadi centres, with its primary focus on children under six and on pregnant and lactating women and an explicit emphasis on the first thousand days from conception to age two. **Analysis:** Connect right or deprivation → institutional duty and eligibility rule → frontline implementation → differentiated access and outcome → grievance, audit or course correction. **Qualification:** State internal group and regional variation, legal/operative status, denominator, fiscal or causal limit, accessibility, dignity, privacy, portability or residual exclusion.

### ORIGINAL MAINS 6 — 20 MARKS

**Question:** Assess whether portability and identification reform have reduced exclusion from India's food entitlement. Answer in about 300 words.

**Model thesis:** Portability addresses one exclusion mechanism while identification reform can generate another, so the assessment must separate inclusion from exclusion error, use the official identification process as evidence, and end with a verdict that carries a stated data limit.

**Claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification:**

- One Nation One Ration Card enables portability of the Act's entitlements across States using Aadhaar-seeded ration cards and electronic point-of-sale machines at fair-price shops, with the technical backbone provided through the national informatics and food-corporation systems, and it is the instrument that addresses the exclusion of migrant and mobile beneficiaries from State-specific lists; it is not a separate ration card and not a separate entitlement but a system interoperability feature, and the owners record that its cumulative transaction count on the integrated management portal rises continuously and is therefore not fixed here, the durable fact being operational inter-State portability.
- Inclusion error places non-eligible persons on distribution lists while exclusion error keeps eligible persons off them, most often through documentation, seeding, migration and disability-related barriers, and the owners record that portability and deduplication aim at both while exclusion risk for migrants and marginalised groups persists, which is why the official portal's description of identification as a continuous process of removing ineligible, fake and duplicate cards and adding births and genuine left-out households is itself examinable. On ownership, three Mains demands from 2018, 2019 and 2024 and two objective demands from 2018 and 2023 are routed to this topic in the audited ledgers, the official answer keys for those objective demands are recorded by the ledgers as unavailable locally, so no option is inferred or recorded, and no poverty, nutrition or coverage figure is asserted anywhere in this package without its issuing body, survey round or policy date.
- The National Food Security Act, 2013 provides a statutory entitlement of five kilograms of foodgrain per person per month for priority households and thirty-five kilograms per household per month for Antyodaya Anna Yojana households, and the Department of Food and Public Distribution's own portal, read on 2 September 2026, records that the Act was enacted on 5 July 2013, that it marks a paradigm shift from a welfare to a rights-based approach, that it legally entitles up to seventy-five per cent of the rural population and fifty per cent of the urban population to receive subsidised foodgrains under the targeted public distribution system, that it is implemented in all States and Union Territories, and that out of a maximum coverage of 81.34 crore persons around 80 crore persons are covered at present, with identification treated as a continuous process of exclusion and inclusion.
- The official portal records that the Act defines a joint responsibility, under which the Centre allocates the required foodgrains to States and Union Territories, transports them up to designated depots in each State and Union Territory and provides central assistance for delivery from designated Food Corporation of India godowns to the doorstep of the fair-price shop, while States and Union Territories are responsible for effective implementation, which includes identifying eligible households, issuing ration cards and distributing the foodgrain entitlement; this division is the reason why a delivery failure must be attributed to a named tier rather than to the government in general.

**Qualified conclusion:** Portability addresses one exclusion mechanism while identification reform can generate another, so the assessment must separate inclusion from exclusion error, use the official identification process as evidence, and end with a verdict that carries a stated data limit.

**Demand decoding:** The directive **assess** requires a direct position on “Assess whether portability and identification reform have reduced exclusion from India's food…”, every clause, constitutional/statutory/policy distinction, rights-holder and institution map, eligibility/exclusion and delivery chain, intersectional and regional variation, named Indian evidence, accountability and qualified conclusion.

> **Subject:** Social Justice | **Tier:** Advanced | **GS Paper:** GS-II;
> GS-III (food security economics), GS-I (population/poverty geography).
> **Core area:** NFSA implementation gaps; Poshan 2.0 architecture; nutrition-
> sensitive vs nutrition-specific interventions; ONORC mechanics; NFHS data
> interpretation.
> **Grounded in:** NFSA, 2013; PMGKAY-NFSA merger and free-foodgrain policy
> (1 Jan 2023, extended to Dec 2028); Poshan 2.0 guidelines (MWCD); PM POSHAN (MoE);
> NFHS-6 (2023-24), released 29 May 2026, with NFHS-5 as comparator;
> 2024 GS-II PYQ Q7.
> ✅ = source-grounded | ⚠️ = inference/analysis | 📰 = current anchor.
> *Companion: `basic/02_Poverty-Hunger-Food-and-Nutrition-Security.md`.*

#### 1. Architecture

```text
FOOD SECURITY (calorie access)              NUTRITION SECURITY (multi-dimensional)
        |                                              |
        v                                              v
   NFSA 2013                                 DIETARY DIVERSITY + MICRONUTRIENTS
   (grain entitlement)                       + CARE PRACTICES + HEALTH/WASH
        |                                              |
        +------------------+   +------------------------+
                           |   |
                           v   v
                   NUTRITION-SPECIFIC          NUTRITION-SENSITIVE
                   INTERVENTIONS               INTERVENTIONS
                   (direct nutrition           (indirect nutrition
                    delivery)                   impact via other
                        |                       sectors)
                        |                           |
           +------------+------------+        +-----+-----+
           |            |            |        |     |     |
           v            v            v        v     v     v
        Poshan 2.0   PM POSHAN   IFA/      WASH   Agri   Social
        (ICDS)       (school     micro-    (SBM,  (diver- protection
                      meal)      nutrient  JJM)   sified  (income
                                 suppl.)          crops)  support)
                           |
                           v
                   FIRST-1000-DAYS WINDOW
                   (pregnancy + first 2 years)
                   - highest return on intervention
                   - prevents irreversible stunting
                           |
                           v
                   OUTCOME: REDUCED STUNTING, WASTING, ANAEMIA
                   (NFHS-tracked indicators)
```

**Analytical claim:** ⚠️ Breaking the poverty-malnutrition-human-capital cycle
(2024 Q7) requires both nutrition-specific interventions (Poshan 2.0, PM POSHAN,
micronutrient supplementation) and nutrition-sensitive convergence (WASH, income
support, agricultural diversification); NFSA grain entitlements address calorie
access but not dietary diversity or the first-1000-days critical window.

#### 2. Concepts and distinctions

| Concept | Precise meaning |
|---|---|
| ✅ **Nutrition-specific interventions** | Directly target nutritional outcomes — e.g., supplementary feeding, micronutrient supplementation, growth monitoring, breastfeeding promotion (Poshan 2.0, PM POSHAN). |
| ✅ **Nutrition-sensitive interventions** | Address underlying determinants of nutrition through other sectors — e.g., sanitation/water (WASH), agriculture diversification, social protection, women's education — impact nutrition indirectly. |
| ✅ **First-1000-days (conception to age 2)** | The critical window during which nutritional deprivation causes irreversible stunting and cognitive impairment; interventions here yield the highest return. |
| ⚠️ **Inclusion error vs exclusion error (in NFSA)** | Inclusion error: non-eligible persons on PDS lists (leakage); exclusion error: eligible persons excluded (often due to documentation/Aadhaar issues) — ONORC and deduplication aim to reduce both, but exclusion risk for migrants and marginalised groups persists. |
| ⚠️ **Take-home ration (THR) vs hot cooked meal (HCM)** | THR: dry rations given to beneficiaries to cook at home (used for PLW, children 6 months-3 years); HCM: cooked meal served at anganwadi/school — HCM ensures consumption at point of delivery but requires infrastructure; THR is more flexible but may be diverted within households. |
| ✅ **Severe Acute Malnutrition (SAM) vs Moderate Acute Malnutrition (MAM)** | SAM: weight-for-height < -3 SD (severe wasting), requires facility-based therapeutic care; MAM: weight-for-height < -2 SD to -3 SD, can be managed through community-based supplementary feeding — both tracked by ICDS/anganwadi growth monitoring. |

#### 3. Detailed causal chain: the intergenerational trap (2024 PYQ Q7)

1. **Poverty** limits household capacity to purchase diverse, nutrient-dense food
   and invest in health/sanitation.
2. **Food insecurity** manifests as calorie deficit (hunger) and dietary
   monotony (lack of micronutrients).
3. **Undernutrition in early childhood** (especially first 1000 days) causes
   stunting (chronic) and wasting (acute), impairs cognitive development and
   weakens immunity.
4. **Impaired development** reduces school attendance, learning outcomes and
   skill acquisition.
5. **Low human capital** translates into low productivity and earnings in
   adulthood.
6. **Low earnings** perpetuate household poverty, which is then transmitted to
   the next generation (intergenerational trap).
7. **Breaking-the-cycle levers:**
   - Nutrition-specific: Poshan 2.0 (first-1000-days focus), PM POSHAN (school-
     age), micronutrient supplementation (IFA tablets).
   - Nutrition-sensitive: WASH convergence (SBM, JJM), income support (DBT,
     social security), agricultural diversification.
   - Portability: ONORC for migrant-worker inclusion.

#### 4. Institutional and reform architecture

- ✅ **NFSA, 2013:** Statutory entitlement; state-wise identification of priority
  households and AAY; coverage ratio (~67% of population) but exclusion errors
  persist for migrants, homeless and those lacking documentation.
- ✅ **Poshan 2.0 / Saksham Anganwadi (MWCD):** Consolidates ICDS, Poshan Abhiyaan
  and related schemes; emphasises convergence, Jan Andolan (community
  mobilisation), ICT-enabled monitoring (Poshan Tracker app) and first-1000-days
  targeting.
- ✅ **PM POSHAN (MoE):** Centrally-sponsored scheme with cost-sharing between
  Centre and states; norms for calorie/protein content, mid-day meal menus,
  kitchen infrastructure and social audit.
- ✅ **ONORC:** Requires Aadhaar seeding of ration cards, ePoS deployment at
  fair-price shops and inter-state data exchange via IMPDS portal; operational
  across all 36 states/UTs since Assam completed the pan-India rollout in June 2022.
- ⚠️ **Poshan Tracker (app):** Real-time data entry by anganwadi workers for
  growth monitoring, supplementary nutrition delivery and service coverage — but
  data quality depends on AWW capacity and device availability.

#### 5. Indian applications and boundary cases

- ✅ **2024 GS-II PYQ Q7:** The question directly tests the vicious-cycle concept
  and expects multi-sectoral levers; a narrow NFSA-only answer misses the
  nutrition-security dimension.
- ⚠️ **ONORC during COVID-19 lockdowns:** Enabled migrant workers stranded outside
  home states to access rations — demonstrated portability value but also exposed
  exclusion of those without Aadhaar seeding.
- ⚠️ **NFHS-5 inter-state variation:** States like Kerala and Tamil Nadu have low
  stunting; states like Bihar and UP have high stunting — illustrates that NFSA
  coverage alone does not explain nutrition outcomes; health infrastructure, WASH
  and women's education matter.
- ⚠️ **Boundary case — fortified rice under NFSA:** Government has mandated
  distribution of fortified rice (iron, folic acid, B12) through PDS — a
  nutrition-specific enhancement within the food-security architecture, but
  effectiveness depends on consumption patterns and bioavailability.

#### 6. Limitations and trade-offs

- ⚠️ **NFSA grain-centricity:** Subsidised rice/wheat addresses calorie access
  but not dietary diversity (pulses, vegetables, fruits, animal-source foods) —
  nutrition security requires complementary measures.
- ⚠️ **Exclusion error vs portability:** ONORC addresses migrant exclusion but
  requires Aadhaar seeding and ePoS connectivity; beneficiaries without
  documentation or in remote areas may still be excluded.
- ⚠️ **Anganwadi workforce capacity:** Poshan 2.0 relies on AWWs/helpers for
  growth monitoring, counselling and supplementary nutrition delivery; low
  honoraria, multi-tasking and vacancies affect service quality.
- ⚠️ **Data lag:** NFHS rounds are periodic (every 4-5 years); administrative
  dashboards (Poshan Tracker) provide high-frequency data but may suffer from
  reporting bias — the "outcome-learning gap" noted in `00_Master-Framework.md`.

#### 7. Must-Know Facts for Advanced Prelims

- ✅ Poshan 2.0 emphasises the "first 1000 days" (conception to age 2) as the
  critical window for preventing stunting and cognitive impairment.
- ✅ NFSA coverage: ~75% rural, ~50% urban populations are entitled to foodgrains;
  the Act's statutory Central Issue Price ceiling is rice ₹3/kg, wheat ₹2/kg,
  coarse grains ₹1/kg, but current policy is free distribution (since 1 Jan 2023,
  extended to Dec 2028).
- ✅ PM POSHAN (erstwhile Mid-Day Meal) targets children in government and
  government-aided schools, Classes I-VIII.
- ✅ ONORC enables ration-card portability via Aadhaar seeding and ePoS; tracked
  on the IMPDS portal.
- ✅ SAM (Severe Acute Malnutrition): weight-for-height < -3 SD; requires
  facility-based therapeutic care (Nutrition Rehabilitation Centres).

#### 8. Advanced Prelims traps

- ❌ Poshan 2.0 is administered by the Ministry of Education. -> Poshan 2.0 is
  administered by MWCD; PM POSHAN is administered by the Ministry of Education.
- ❌ NFSA provides universal coverage to all citizens. -> NFSA covers ~67% of the
  population (priority households + AAY) via state-wise identification, not
  universal coverage.
- ❌ NFSA beneficiaries currently pay the Act's statutory Central Issue Prices
  (₹3/kg rice, ₹2/kg wheat, ₹1/kg coarse grains). -> Those prices remain the
  statutory ceiling in the Act, but since 1 January 2023 the PMGKAY-NFSA merger
  has made NFSA foodgrains free of cost for AAY/priority-household beneficiaries,
  a policy the Union Cabinet extended through December 2028.

#### 9. 📰 Current-anchor note

- 📰 **Survey-status check (29 May 2026):** NFHS-6 (2023-24) is released; reported
  under-five stunting is 29.3%, against 35.5% in NFHS-5. Retain each survey round and
  do not splice indicators from different rounds into one baseline.
- 📰 **Free-foodgrain policy:** NFSA foodgrains have been distributed free of cost
  to AAY/priority-household beneficiaries since 1 January 2023 (PMGKAY merged
  into NFSA, replacing the earlier ₹1-3/kg Central Issue Price payment), and the
  Union Cabinet extended this free-distribution policy for five years, through
  December 2028, at an approved outlay of roughly ₹11.8 lakh crore covering
  roughly 81 crore beneficiaries.
- 📰 Fortified rice distribution under NFSA was mandated for phased national
  rollout; the durable policy fact is the government's fortification mandate, not
  a specific coverage percentage that changes as rollout progresses.

#### 10. PYQ-based analytical application

- ✅ **2024 Q7 (10 marks):** A strong answer should:
  1. Diagram the vicious cycle (poverty -> undernutrition -> impaired development
     -> low productivity -> poverty).
  2. Distinguish nutrition-specific (Poshan 2.0, PM POSHAN, micronutrient
     supplementation) from nutrition-sensitive (WASH, income support, agriculture)
     interventions.
  3. Emphasise the first-1000-days window and women's nutrition (anaemia in
     adolescent girls and PLW).
  4. Cite ONORC portability for migrant inclusion.
  5. Conclude with the convergence imperative: no single scheme breaks the cycle.

- ✅ **2025 GS-II Q16 (15 marks, 250 words):** Build the “paradox of poverty” as a
  self-reinforcing asset-capability loop:

```text
UNEQUAL OWNERSHIP OF LAND / CAPITAL / CREDIT / DIGITAL ACCESS
                         |
                         v
       unequal opportunity and bargaining power
                         |
                         v
             low capability and income
                         |
                         v
       weak saving, investment and asset formation
                         |
                         +----> inherited ownership inequality
```

  Separate productive-asset reform (land records/tenancy security, credit, skills,
  commons and digital access) from necessary but non-transformative consumption relief.

#### 11. Mains-ready framework

**Central thesis:** The poverty-malnutrition-human-capital cycle is
intergenerational and multi-causal; breaking it requires nutrition-specific
interventions (Poshan 2.0 for the first 1000 days, PM POSHAN for school-age,
micronutrient supplementation) combined with nutrition-sensitive convergence
(WASH, income support, agricultural diversification) and portability for migrants
(ONORC) — NFSA grain entitlements are necessary but not sufficient.

1. Define the vicious cycle with the intergenerational-trap logic.
2. Distinguish food security (calorie access) from nutrition security (dietary
   diversity, micronutrients, care, WASH).
3. Map nutrition-specific vs nutrition-sensitive interventions.
4. Identify the first-1000-days window as the highest-return intervention point.
5. Acknowledge implementation gaps (exclusion error, AWW capacity, data lag).
6. Recommend convergence with a specific, measurable outcome target (e.g.,
   reduce stunting by X percentage points in Y years via coordinated Poshan 2.0
   + SBM + JJM action).

#### 12. Probable questions

- ✅ **Mains (10 marks, 2024 Q7):** Poverty and malnutrition create a vicious
  cycle, adversely affecting human capital formation. What steps can be taken to
  break the cycle?
- ✅ **Mains (15 marks, 2025 GS-II Q16):** “Inequality in the ownership pattern of
  resources is one of the major causes of poverty. Discuss in the context of ‘paradox of
  poverty’.”
- ⚠️ **Prelims:** Which of the following is administered by the Ministry of Women
  and Child Development — PM POSHAN, Poshan 2.0, ONORC or NFSA?
- ⚠️ **Mains (15 marks):** Distinguish nutrition-specific from nutrition-sensitive
  interventions. How does India's policy architecture attempt to integrate both
  for improved nutritional outcomes?
- ⚠️ **Mains (15 marks):** "ONORC is a necessary but not sufficient condition for
  ensuring food and nutrition security for migrant workers." Discuss.
